<div class="ltx_page_main">
<div class="ltx_page_content">
<article class="ltx_document ltx_authors_1line">
<h1 class="ltx_title ltx_title_document">IdeaBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models for Research Idea Generation</h1>
<div class="ltx_authors">
<span class="ltx_creator ltx_role_author">
<span class="ltx_personname">Sikun Guo
</span></span>
<span class="ltx_author_before">  </span><span class="ltx_creator ltx_role_author">
<span class="ltx_personname">Amir Hassan Shariatmadari
</span></span>
<span class="ltx_author_before">  </span><span class="ltx_creator ltx_role_author">
<span class="ltx_personname">Guangzhi Xiong
</span></span>
<span class="ltx_author_before">  </span><span class="ltx_creator ltx_role_author">
<span class="ltx_personname">Albert Huang
</span></span>
<span class="ltx_author_before">  </span><span class="ltx_creator ltx_role_author">
<span class="ltx_personname">Eric Xie
</span></span>
<span class="ltx_author_before">  </span><span class="ltx_creator ltx_role_author">
<span class="ltx_personname">Stefan Bekiranov
</span></span>
<span class="ltx_author_before">  </span><span class="ltx_creator ltx_role_author">
<span class="ltx_personname">Aidong Zhang
</span></span></div>

<div id="abstract1" class="ltx_abstract"><h6 class="ltx_title ltx_title_abstract">Abstract</h6>
    
<p id="abstract1.1" class="ltx_p">Large Language Models (LLMs) have transformed how people interact with artificial intelligence (AI) systems, achieving state-of-the-art results in various tasks, including scientific discovery and hypothesis generation. However, the lack of a comprehensive and systematic evaluation framework for generating research ideas using LLMs poses a significant obstacle to understanding and assessing their generative capabilities in scientific discovery. To address this gap, we propose <span id="abstract1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">IdeaBench</span>, a benchmark system that includes a comprehensive dataset and an evaluation framework for standardizing the assessment of research idea generation using LLMs. Our dataset comprises titles and abstracts from a diverse range of influential papers, along with their referenced works. To emulate the human process of generating research ideas, we profile LLMs as domain-specific researchers and ground them in the same context considered by human researchers. This maximizes the utilization of the LLMs’ parametric knowledge to dynamically generate new research ideas. We also introduce an evaluation framework for assessing the quality of generated research ideas. Our evaluation framework is a two-stage process: first, using GPT-4o to rank ideas based on user-specified quality indicators such as novelty and feasibility, enabling scalable personalization; and second, calculating relative ranking based “Insight Score” to quantify the chosen quality indicator. The proposed benchmark system will be a valuable asset for the community to measure and compare different LLMs, ultimately advancing the automation of the scientific discovery process. Our code and dataset are available at: <span class="ltx_ref ltx_nolink ltx_url ltx_ref_self">https://anonymous.4open.science/r/IdeaBench-2747/</span>.</p>
  
</div>
<section id="Sx1" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section">Introduction</h2>

<div id="Sx1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Recent years have witnessed the rapid development of Large Language Models (LLMs). LLMs like GPT-4 <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib14" title="" class="ltx_ref">OpenAI 2023</a>)</cite> and LLama series <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib19" title="" class="ltx_ref">Touvron et al. 2023</a>)</cite> introduced advanced capabilities that set them apart from previous generations of machine learning models. Among these capabilities, in-context learning allows LLMs to understand and respond to user prompts in a nuanced manner without requiring additional training for each specific task, enabling LLMs to generalize across a wide range of tasks, providing robust state-of-the-art performance even with limited data <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib4" title="" class="ltx_ref">Brown et al. 2020</a>)</cite>. As a result, LLMs have revolutionized the way humans interact with AI systems, making it possible to generate coherent text, translate languages, answer questions, and even compose creative content with unprecedented accuracy and fluency <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib5" title="" class="ltx_ref">Bubeck et al. 2023</a>)</cite>. The impact of these advancements extends beyond consumer applications, influencing various sophisticated domains such as education <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib13" title="" class="ltx_ref">Moore et al. 2023</a>)</cite>, healthcare <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib24" title="" class="ltx_ref">Yang et al. 2023a</a>)</cite>, and scientific research <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib23" title="" class="ltx_ref">Wysocki et al. 2024</a>)</cite>.</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx1.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx1.p2.1" class="ltx_p">Recently, the impressive performance of LLMs in everyday applications has sparked significant interest in academia, particularly for their potential use in scientific discovery or hypothesis generation <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib1" title="" class="ltx_ref">AI4Science and Quantum 2023</a>)</cite>. Several studies have explored leveraging LLMs to generate hypotheses or research ideas <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib25" title="" class="ltx_ref">Yang et al. 2023b</a>; <a href="#bib.bib22" title="" class="ltx_ref">Wang et al. 2023b</a>; <a href="#bib.bib29" title="" class="ltx_ref">Zhou et al. 2024</a>; <a href="#bib.bib2" title="" class="ltx_ref">Baek et al. 2024</a>; <a href="#bib.bib16" title="" class="ltx_ref">Qiu et al. 2023</a>)</cite>. However, despite numerous results, a unified and comprehensive framework for evaluating generated research ideas is still lacking, making it difficult for the community to clearly understand the performance spectrum of different techniques for generating research ideas.</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx1.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx1.p3.1" class="ltx_p">To address this limitation, we introduce a standardized evaluation framework designed to emulate how human researchers generate research ideas. This framework, termed <span id="Sx1.p3.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">IdeaBench</span>, comprises three main components: dataset construction, research idea generation, and a novel metric to evaluate the quality of the generated research ideas. The intuition behind this framework is grounded in the typical research process of how researchers generate new scientific research ideas as described below:</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx1.p4" class="ltx_para">
<ol id="Sx1.I1" class="ltx_enumerate">
<li id="Sx1.I1.i1" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">1.</span> 
<div id="Sx1.I1.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx1.I1.i1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Targeting a specific topic.</p>
</div></li>
<li id="Sx1.I1.i2" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">2.</span> 
<div id="Sx1.I1.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx1.I1.i2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Reviewing related literature, focusing on recent findings and methodologies.</p>
</div></li>
<li id="Sx1.I1.i3" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">3.</span> 
<div id="Sx1.I1.i3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx1.I1.i3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Identifying gaps in knowledge or methods within these recent findings.</p>
</div></li>
<li id="Sx1.I1.i4" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">4.</span> 
<div id="Sx1.I1.i4.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx1.I1.i4.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Proposing research ideas to address these gaps.</p>
</div></li>
</ol>
</div>
<div id="Sx1.p5" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx1.p5.1" class="ltx_p">We first construct a benchmark dataset that includes meticulously filtered 2,374 target papers’
abstracts
from biomedical research fields. These target papers serve as the ground-truth sources of research ideas. Additionally, the dataset contains the
abstracts of the papers referenced by the target papers, providing the context necessary for LLMs to generate relevant research ideas. This comprehensive dataset aims to capture the complexity and specificity of scientific research, particularly in the biomedical domain, thus offering a solid foundation for evaluating LLMs’ capability in generating research ideas.</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx1.p6" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx1.p6.1" class="ltx_p">Based on the benchmark dataset, we design a prompt template that leverages LLMs to generate research ideas. In addition to grounding the context for idea generation using reference papers from our dataset, we also profile the LLMs as domain-specific researchers in the prompt. This approach aims to dynamically maximize the utilization of the LLMs’ parametric knowledge, enabling the generation of more in-depth and insightful research ideas. It can also be used as a baseline for future comparisons.</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx1.p7" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx1.p7.1" class="ltx_p">To accurately assess the quality of generated research ideas, we design an evaluation framework which incorporates two critical components: personalized quality ranking and relative quality scoring. This dual approach allows for a nuanced assessment that takes into account user-defined quality indicators such as novelty, feasibility, etc. Our design ensures a versatile and comprehensive evaluation framework, capable of adapting to different research contexts and providing meaningful insights into the quality of LLM-generated ideas.
Our results show that recent high-capacity LLMs are capable of generating research ideas using <span id="Sx1.p7.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">IdeaBench</span> dataset, and our metric is able to assess the quality of generated research ideas from different dimensions. We hope this work inspires academia to further unleash the potential of LLMs in supporting research ideation, ultimately accelerating scientific discovery in the future.</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx1.p8" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx1.p8.1" class="ltx_p">To summarize, our contributions are as follows:</p>
<ul id="Sx1.I2" class="ltx_itemize">
<li id="Sx1.I2.i1" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span> 
<div id="Sx1.I2.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx1.I2.i1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We construct <span id="Sx1.I2.i1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">IdeaBench</span> dataset, which consists of 2,374 influential biomedical target papers along with their 29,408 reference papers, to evaluate LLMs’ capabilities in generating research ideas.</p>
</div></li>
<li id="Sx1.I2.i2" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span> 
<div id="Sx1.I2.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx1.I2.i2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We propose an evaluation framework which offers a scalable and versatile metric called “Insight Score”, which can quantify novelty, feasibility, or any other quality indicators defined by human researchers.</p>
</div></li>
<li id="Sx1.I2.i3" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span> 
<div id="Sx1.I2.i3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx1.I2.i3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We conduct extensive experiments to demonstrate several LLMs’ abilities in generating research ideas based on our dataset and evaluation metric.</p>
</div></li>
</ul>
</div>
</section>
<section id="Sx2" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section">Related work</h2>

<section id="Sx2.SS0.SSSx1" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">Machine Learning for Hypothesis Generation.</h4>

<div id="Sx2.SS0.SSSx1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx2.SS0.SSSx1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Most existing research on hypothesis generation has concentrated on literature-based discovery (LBD), aiming to predict pairwise relationships between discrete concepts <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib21" title="" class="ltx_ref">Wang et al. 2023a</a>)</cite>. This approach involves uncovering new scientific knowledge by mining literature to identify meaningful implicit associations between unrelated biomedical concepts. The majority of prior studies have focused on identifying these implicit connections from snapshots of the corpus. While these LBD-based approaches are accurate and verifiable, they assume that all concepts are known beforehand and need only to be connected, without considering the contextual factors that human scientists incorporate during ideation. Moreover, these methods do not address the inductive and generative nature of scientific inquiry. Recently, several new studies have explored the use of large language models (LLMs) for hypothesis generation. For instance, in <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib22" title="" class="ltx_ref">Wang et al. 2023b</a>)</cite>, the authors presented a framework called SciMON that leverages past scientific literature as context for fine-tuning LLMs for hypothesis generation. MOOSE <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib25" title="" class="ltx_ref">Yang et al. 2023b</a>)</cite> utilized multi-level LLM self-feedback to boost scientific hypotheses discovery in social science. ResearchAgent <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib2" title="" class="ltx_ref">Baek et al. 2024</a>)</cite> employed LLMs to automatically generate and refine problems, methods, and experiment designs starting with a core paper and entity-centric knowledge graphs. <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib29" title="" class="ltx_ref">Zhou et al. 2024</a>)</cite> proposed a prompting approach to iteratively generate hypotheses using LLMs based on training examples.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="Sx2.SS0.SSSx2" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">Evaluation for Open-ended Text Generation.</h4>

<div id="Sx2.SS0.SSSx2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx2.SS0.SSSx2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Although human judgment is still considered the golden standard for evaluating open-ended text generation, the Natural Language Processing community has tried to develop different approaches to approximate human evaluation in a scalable way. Traditional metrics like BLEU <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib15" title="" class="ltx_ref">Papineni et al. 2002</a>)</cite> and ROUGE <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib11" title="" class="ltx_ref">Lin 2004</a>)</cite> measure the lexical overlap between model generated content and ground-truth reference. Later on, several efforts use pre-trained language models to measure distributional similarity <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib27" title="" class="ltx_ref">Zhang et al. 2019</a>; <a href="#bib.bib28" title="" class="ltx_ref">Zhao et al. 2019</a>)</cite> or token probabilities <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib26" title="" class="ltx_ref">Yuan, Neubig, and Liu 2021</a>; <a href="#bib.bib18" title="" class="ltx_ref">Thompson and Post 2020</a>)</cite>. With the increasing popularity and impressive performance of Large Language Models, recent endeavors employ LLMs as autoraters for open-ended text generation <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib6" title="" class="ltx_ref">Chiang and Lee 2023</a>; <a href="#bib.bib12" title="" class="ltx_ref">Liu et al. 2023</a>; <a href="#bib.bib5" title="" class="ltx_ref">Bubeck et al. 2023</a>; <a href="#bib.bib3" title="" class="ltx_ref">Bai et al. 2024</a>; <a href="#bib.bib7" title="" class="ltx_ref">Fu et al. 2024</a>; <a href="#bib.bib20" title="" class="ltx_ref">Vu et al. 2024</a>)</cite>, the effectiveness of using LLMs as autoraters is often reflected by its correlation with human-ratings, making autoraters a promising alternative to human evaluators for large-scale evaluation.</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>
<section id="Sx3" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section">Methodology</h2>

<div id="Sx3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">In this section, we introduce the details of the three components of our framework, namely, dataset construction, research idea generation, and evaluation of the generated ideas. The first component is to collect a set of valuable target papers and reference papers so that the reference papers can be used to generate new research ideas and compare with those in the target papers. The second component is to design an LLM prompt tailored for generating research ideas,
and the last component is to formulate an evaluation metric to measure the quality of the generated ideas.</p>
</div>
<section id="Sx3.SSx1" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection">Dataset Construction</h3>

<div id="Sx3.SSx1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.SSx1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">The dataset construction consists of two components: curating a set of valuable papers which will be used as the target papers, and accumulating the reference papers which were used to generate ideas in the target papers.</p>
</div>
<section id="Sx3.SSx1.SSSx1" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">Data Collection.</h4>

<div id="Sx3.SSx1.SSSx1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.SSx1.SSSx1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">To create a benchmark dataset for evaluating the research idea generation capabilities of LLMs, we meticulously curated a set of high-quality biomedical primary research papers published in 2024. Our goal is to construct a dataset that accurately reflects the state-of-the-art in the field and provides a robust foundation for evaluating LLMs’ capabilities for generating research ideas. Motivated by our desire to include only high-quality, peer-reviewed research, as well as those recognized by the scientific community through citations, we retrieve papers either from top venues or from other venues but are recognized by a significant number of citations. We use the Semantic Scholar API <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib9" title="" class="ltx_ref">Kinney et al. 2023</a>)</cite> to retrieve all biomedical papers published in top biomedical conferences according to Google Scholar venue rankings <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib8" title="" class="ltx_ref">Google Scholar 2024</a>)</cite> in the year 2024 with at least one citation. We also retrieve papers published from other biomedical venues in the year 2024 that have at least 20 citations. Any duplicate papers are removed. We refer to these selected papers as target papers in which the ground-truth research ideas lie.</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx3.SSx1.SSSx1.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.SSx1.SSSx1.p2.1" class="ltx_p">To further enrich our dataset and provide context, we also extracted the reference papers cited by these target papers. This is done using the Semantic Scholar API as well. These reference papers contain the foundational ideas that motivated the research in the target papers, offering valuable insights into the background and rationale behind each study. By mapping each target paper to its corresponding set of reference papers, we create a comprehensive contextual framework that can aid LLMs in generating coherent and relevant research ideas.
Also, to ensure the completeness and usability of our dataset, we disregard papers with critical missing information, such as abstracts.
This is crucial for maintaining the integrity of our evaluation, as missing information or poor contextualization could hinder LLMs in understanding the main ideas and prevent fair comparisons with generated research ideas.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="Sx3.SSx1.SSSx2" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">Relevance and Significance Based Reference Filtering</h4>

<div id="Sx3.SSx1.SSSx2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.SSx1.SSSx2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We believe that the reference papers provide the most significant information for generating the new research ideas in the target papers. However, not all references cited in a paper are equally relevant to its central theme. Especially when computing resources are limited, it’s vital to focus on the most pertinent and significant references in the target papers. Our motivation for implementing a significance-relevancy-based filtering process is to ensure that the reference papers align closely with the target paper’s primary research ideas, thus maximizing the relevance and utility of the information provided to the LLMs.
To enhance the relevance of the reference papers, we propose a filtering process that prioritizes references directly contributing to the main research idea of the target paper. This approach excludes irrelevant or overly specific references that do not align with the overarching research theme, thereby optimizing the dataset for the generation of new research ideas under constrained resources.</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx3.SSx1.SSSx2.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.SSx1.SSSx2.p2.1" class="ltx_p">The filtering process is guided by three conditions:</p>
<ol id="Sx3.I3" class="ltx_enumerate">
<li id="Sx3.I3.i1" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">1.</span> 
<div id="Sx3.I3.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.I3.i1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Sx3.I3.i1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Citation Count Threshold</span>. We exclude reference papers with fewer than five citations to ensure the inclusion of high-quality, widely recognized references.</p>
</div></li>
<li id="Sx3.I3.i2" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">2.</span> 
<div id="Sx3.I3.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.I3.i2.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Sx3.I3.i2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Non-Primary Research Exclusion</span>. We remove non-primary research references, such as reviews, editorials, letters, or books, as labeled by Semantic Scholar. These sources often contain diverse ideas not directly relevant to the target paper’s core research.</p>
</div></li>
<li id="Sx3.I3.i3" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">3.</span> 
<div id="Sx3.I3.i3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.I3.i3.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Sx3.I3.i3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Background Section Relevance.</span> We also exclude reference papers that are not cited in the background section of the target paper, as they are less likely to contribute directly to the target paper’s research idea.</p>
</div></li>
</ol>
</div>
<div id="Sx3.SSx1.SSSx2.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.SSx1.SSSx2.p3.1" class="ltx_p">This filtering process ensures that the LLMs are provided with highly relevant and focused information, facilitating the generation of new and meaningful research ideas. We will use random filtering as a baseline, and the effectiveness of our filtering method will be further discussed in the ablation study section. Our approach aims to strike a balance between resource efficiency and the richness of information, thereby advancing the quality of research idea generation.</p>
</div>
<figure id="Sx3.F1" class="ltx_figure"><span class="ltx_inline-block"><svg id="Sx3.F1.pic1" class="ltx_picture" height="194.84" overflow="visible" version="1.1" viewBox="0 0 700 194.84" width="700"><g style="--ltx-stroke-color:#000000;--ltx-fill-color:#000000;" fill="#000000" stroke="#000000" stroke-width="0.4pt" transform="translate(0,194.84) matrix(1 0 0 -1 0 0)"><g style="--ltx-fill-color:#000000;" fill="#000000" fill-opacity="1.0"><path style="stroke:none" d="M 0 5.91 L 0 188.93 C 0 192.19 2.64 194.84 5.91 194.84 L 694.09 194.84 C 697.36 194.84 700 192.19 700 188.93 L 700 5.91 C 700 2.64 697.36 0 694.09 0 L 5.91 0 C 2.64 0 0 2.64 0 5.91 Z"></path></g><g style="--ltx-fill-color:#D9D9D9;" fill="#D9D9D9" fill-opacity="1.0"><path style="stroke:none" d="M 1.97 5.91 L 1.97 170.73 L 698.03 170.73 L 698.03 5.91 C 698.03 3.73 696.27 1.97 694.09 1.97 L 5.91 1.97 C 3.73 1.97 1.97 3.73 1.97 5.91 Z"></path></g><g fill-opacity="1.0" transform="matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 21.65 179.32)"><foreignObject style="--ltx-fo-width:47.46em;--ltx-fo-height:0.69em;--ltx-fo-depth:0.19em;font-size:10pt;" height="12.3" overflow="visible" transform="matrix(1 0 0 -1 0 9.61)" width="656.71"><span class="ltx_foreignobject_container"><span class="ltx_foreignobject_content">
<span id="Sx3.F1.pic1.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_minipage ltx_align_bottom" style="width:47.46em;">
<span id="Sx3.F1.pic1.1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Sx3.F1.pic1.1.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#FFFFFF;">Prompt template for generating research ideas</span></span>

You are tasked with creating a hypothesis or research idea given some background knowledge. The background knowledge is provided by abstracts from other papers. 
<br class="ltx_break"></span></span>
<span id="Sx3.F1.pic1.2.2" class="ltx_p"><span id="Sx3.F1.pic1.2.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#000000;">Here are the abstracts:
<br class="ltx_break"></span></span>
<span id="Sx3.F1.pic1.2.3" class="ltx_p"><span id="Sx3.F1.pic1.2.3.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#000000;">Abstract 1:<code id="Sx3.F1.pic1.2.3.1.1" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{reference_paper_1_abstract}</code> 
<br class="ltx_break">Abstract 2:<code id="Sx3.F1.pic1.2.3.1.2" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{reference_paper_2_abstract}</code> 
<br class="ltx_break">…… 
<br class="ltx_break">Abstract n:<code id="Sx3.F1.pic1.2.3.1.3" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{reference_paper_n_abstract}</code> 
<br class="ltx_break"></span></span>
<span id="Sx3.F1.pic1.2.4" class="ltx_p"><span id="Sx3.F1.pic1.2.4.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#000000;">Using these abstracts, reason over them and come up with a novel hypothesis. Please avoid copying ideas directly, rather use the insights to inspire a novel hypothesis in the form of a brief and concise paragraph.</span></span>
</span></span></span></foreignObject></g></g></svg></span>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure">Figure 1: </span>Prompt template used to generate research ideas.</figcaption>
</figure>
</section>
</section>
<section id="Sx3.SSx2" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection">Research Idea Generation</h3>

<div id="Sx3.SSx2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.SSx2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">In the process of generating a research idea, human scientists rely on relevant background information, typically reflected in the references cited in their published work. To harness the capabilities of LLMs for generating research ideas, we adopt a similar approach by grounding the LLMs in the same context considered by human researchers. Our motivation for this is to emulate human thought processes in LLMs, ensuring that the generated ideas are informed and contextually relevant. Providing LLMs with related information or context is crucial; without it, the models may struggle to meaningfully connect relevant parametric knowledge learned from their pretraining corpus.</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx3.SSx2.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.SSx2.p2.1" class="ltx_p">To achieve this, the abstract of each target paper encapsulates the primary research idea developed by human researchers, while the abstracts of the reference papers contain the key ideas considered during the formulation of these main research ideas. For each target paper, we prompt the LLM with the abstracts of the reference papers as background information. This is accompanied by a specially designed prompt to guide the generation of new research ideas. This process is illustrated in Figure <a href="#Sx3.F1" title="Figure 1 ‣ Relevance and Significance Based Reference Filtering ‣ Dataset Construction ‣ Methodology ‣ IdeaBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models for Research Idea Generation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>, where all the <code id="Sx3.SSx2.p2.1.1" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{reference_paper_x_abstract}</code> placeholders are instantiated with the corresponding abstracts of the reference papers.
We profile the LLMs as biomedical researchers at the beginning of the prompt. This facilitates the model’s access to biomedicine-related and research-specific parametric knowledge learned from the pre-training corpus. By profiling the LLMs as biomedical researchers, we aim to maximize the utilization of the model’s parametric knowledge in the biomedical domain, thereby enhancing the relevance and depth of the generated research ideas. The research ideas generated by the LLMs are then compared to the research idea presented in the target paper’s abstract for evaluation.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="Sx3.SSx3" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection">Evaluation of the Generated Ideas</h3>

<div id="Sx3.SSx3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.SSx3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">A straightforward approach to evaluate the quality of generated ideas is to measure the semantic similarity between the generated ideas and the idea from the target paper. However, a similarity-only metric may fail to capture the nuanced qualities of ideas generated by LLMs, such as novelty and feasibility. To address this, we develop a metric called the “Insight Score”, which goes beyond a similarity-only approach to assess the quality of generated ideas in a scalable and rigorous manner. The core of our metric is a personalized quality ranking which allows the users to specify any quality indicators, such as novelty, feasibility, etc. By combining personalized quality rankings with the number of generated ideas, our metric provides a nuanced measurement for various quality indicators, effectively highlighting the strengths and areas for improvement in LLMs’ ability to generate research ideas. The components of our evaluation framework are detailed in the following subsections.</p>
</div>
<section id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx1" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">Personalized Quality Ranking for Generated Research Ideas.</h4>

<div id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">The first step in our evaluation framework involves a personalized quality ranking. For a given target paper and reference papers pair, we first create an idea set that includes both the generated ideas and the original idea from the target paper. Details on how the original idea is extracted from the target paper are provided in the Appendix. Then we use GPT-4o to rank the quality of these ideas based on user-specified quality indicators, without revealing which idea is the original from the target paper. The motivation behind this approach is to provide a flexible and tailored assessment that aligns with the specific interests of human researchers.</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx1.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx1.p2.1" class="ltx_p">The prompt template used to achieve this is shown in Figure <a href="#Sx3.F2" title="Figure 2 ‣ Personalized Quality Ranking for Generated Research Ideas. ‣ Evaluation of the Generated Ideas ‣ Methodology ‣ IdeaBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models for Research Idea Generation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>. In the template, placeholders, denoted by curly brankets <code id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx1.p2.1.1" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{}</code> allow the system to adapt to different scenarios. For instance, if a user wishes to rank research ideas based on their novelty, the system replaces <code id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx1.p2.1.2" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{quality_indicator}</code> with “novelty” in the prompt. Similarly, <code id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx1.p2.1.3" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{target_paper_idea}</code> is replaced with the target paper’s research idea, and <code id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx1.p2.1.4" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{generated_idea_1}</code>, … , <code id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx1.p2.1.5" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{generated_idea_n}</code> are replaced with generated research ideas. The flexibility of this approach allows other quality indicators, such as feasibility, clarity, ethics, etc., to be used to rank research ideas.</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx1.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx1.p3.1" class="ltx_p">Furthermore, fueled by the impressive in-context-learning ability <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib10" title="" class="ltx_ref">Kojima et al. 2022</a>)</cite> of LLMs, the system is able to accommodate a more nuanced understanding of quality indicators held in <code id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx1.p3.1.1" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{quality_indicator}</code>. For example, Bob may define “novelty” as “developing new methodologies, techniques, or instruments that allow researchers to explore questions in ways that were not possible before,” while Alice might consider “novelty” as “applying existing knowledge or technologies to address new problems or in new contexts.” The system allows them to instantiate <code id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx1.p3.1.2" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{quality_indicator}</code> with their respective definitions, ensuring the ranking reflects their specific interpretations. Personalized quality ranking ensures that the evaluation is aligned with the user’s perspective, providing a more accurate and meaningful assessment of the generated research ideas. Additionally, by not disclosing which idea is from the target paper, the system ensures a fair and unbiased ranking of all ideas.</p>
</div>
<figure id="Sx3.F2" class="ltx_figure"><span class="ltx_inline-block"><svg id="Sx3.F2.pic1" class="ltx_picture" height="262.02" overflow="visible" version="1.1" viewBox="0 0 700 262.02" width="700"><g style="--ltx-stroke-color:#000000;--ltx-fill-color:#000000;" fill="#000000" stroke="#000000" stroke-width="0.4pt" transform="translate(0,262.02) matrix(1 0 0 -1 0 0)"><g style="--ltx-fill-color:#000000;" fill="#000000" fill-opacity="1.0"><path style="stroke:none" d="M 0 5.91 L 0 256.12 C 0 259.38 2.64 262.02 5.91 262.02 L 694.09 262.02 C 697.36 262.02 700 259.38 700 256.12 L 700 5.91 C 700 2.64 697.36 0 694.09 0 L 5.91 0 C 2.64 0 0 2.64 0 5.91 Z"></path></g><g style="--ltx-fill-color:#D9D9D9;" fill="#D9D9D9" fill-opacity="1.0"><path style="stroke:none" d="M 1.97 5.91 L 1.97 237.91 L 698.03 237.91 L 698.03 5.91 C 698.03 3.73 696.27 1.97 694.09 1.97 L 5.91 1.97 C 3.73 1.97 1.97 3.73 1.97 5.91 Z"></path></g><g fill-opacity="1.0" transform="matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 21.65 246.51)"><foreignObject style="--ltx-fo-width:47.46em;--ltx-fo-height:0.69em;--ltx-fo-depth:0.19em;font-size:10pt;" height="12.3" overflow="visible" transform="matrix(1 0 0 -1 0 9.61)" width="656.71"><span class="ltx_foreignobject_container"><span class="ltx_foreignobject_content">
<span id="Sx3.F2.pic1.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_minipage ltx_align_bottom" style="width:47.46em;">
<span id="Sx3.F2.pic1.1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Sx3.F2.pic1.1.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#FFFFFF;">Prompt template used to rank research ideas based on user specified quality indicators</span></span>
</span></span></span></foreignObject></g><g fill-opacity="1.0" transform="matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 21.65 17.24)"><foreignObject style="--ltx-fo-width:47.46em;--ltx-fo-height:15.09em;--ltx-fo-depth:0.25em;font-size:10pt;" height="212.32" overflow="visible" transform="matrix(1 0 0 -1 0 208.86)" width="656.71"><span class="ltx_foreignobject_container"><span class="ltx_foreignobject_content">
<span id="Sx3.F2.pic1.2" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_minipage ltx_align_bottom" style="width:47.46em;">
<span id="Sx3.F2.pic1.2.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Sx3.F2.pic1.2.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#000000;">You are a reviewer tasked with ranking the quality of a set of research ideas based on their <code id="Sx3.F2.pic1.2.1.1.1" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{quality_indicator}</code>. The idea with the highest <code id="Sx3.F2.pic1.2.1.1.2" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{quality_indicator}</code> should be ranked first. 
<br class="ltx_break"></span></span>
<span id="Sx3.F2.pic1.2.2" class="ltx_p"><span id="Sx3.F2.pic1.2.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#000000;">Please rank the following hypotheses in the format:

<br class="ltx_break">2. Hypothesis (insert number):(insert brief rationale) 
<br class="ltx_break">3. Hypothesis (insert number):(insert brief rationale) 
<br class="ltx_break">…… 
<br class="ltx_break">n. Hypothesis (insert number):(insert brief rationale) 
<br class="ltx_break"></span></span>
<span id="Sx3.F2.pic1.2.3" class="ltx_p"><span id="Sx3.F2.pic1.2.3.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#000000;">Please rank the following hypotheses:
<br class="ltx_break">Hypothesis 1:
<code id="Sx3.F2.pic1.2.3.1.1" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{target_paper_idea}</code>
<br class="ltx_break">Hypothesis 2:
<code id="Sx3.F2.pic1.2.3.1.2" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{generated_idea_1}</code>
<br class="ltx_break">Hypothesis 3:
<code id="Sx3.F2.pic1.2.3.1.3" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{generated_idea_2}</code>
<br class="ltx_break">…… 
<br class="ltx_break">Hypothesis n:
<code id="Sx3.F2.pic1.2.3.1.4" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{generated_idea_n}</code>
<br class="ltx_break"></span></span>
</span></span></span></foreignObject></g></g></svg></span>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure">Figure 2: </span>Prompt template used to rank research ideas based on user specified quality indicators.</figcaption>
</figure>
</section>
<section id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">Relative Quality Scoring for Generated Research Ideas.</h4>

<div id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">The second step in our evaluation framework is relative quality scoring, which builds upon the personalized quality ranking. The position of the target paper’s idea within the ranked list of research ideas indicates the quality of the generated ideas with respect to the specified quality indicators. Intuitively, if the target paper’s idea ranks higher on the list, it suggests that the generated ideas are of lower quality compared to the target paper’s idea. Conversely, if the generated ideas rank higher than the target paper’s idea, it indicates that the LLM is capable of producing ideas that may be of better quality than those in the target papers. To quantify different quality indicators, we introduce the following notations:</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p2" class="ltx_para">
<ul id="Sx3.I4" class="ltx_itemize">
<li id="Sx3.I4.i1" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span> 
<div id="Sx3.I4.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.I4.i1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><math id="Sx3.I4.i1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>m</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m</annotation></semantics></math>: the number of target papers in our dataset.</p>
</div></li>
<li id="Sx3.I4.i2" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span> 
<div id="Sx3.I4.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.I4.i2.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><math id="Sx3.I4.i2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>n</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n</annotation></semantics></math>: the number of research ideas an LLM generates per query.</p>
</div></li>
<li id="Sx3.I4.i3" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span> 
<div id="Sx3.I4.i3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.I4.i3.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><math id="Sx3.I4.i3.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r_{\text{target}_{i}}|_{q}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><msub><mtext>target</mtext><mi>i</mi></msub></msub><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mi>q</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r_{\text{target}_{i}}|_{q}</annotation></semantics></math>: the <math id="Sx3.I4.i3.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>th target paper’s idea’s rank within the corresponding ranked list of ideas given quality indicator <math id="Sx3.I4.i3.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="q" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>q</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">q</annotation></semantics></math>. When <math id="Sx3.I4.i3.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>n</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n</annotation></semantics></math> ideas are generated, <math id="Sx3.I4.i3.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r_{\text{target}_{i}}|_{q}\in\{1,...,n+1\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><msub><mtext>target</mtext><mi>i</mi></msub></msub><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mi>q</mi></msub><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r_{\text{target}_{i}}|_{q}\in\{1,...,n+1\}</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p3.1" class="ltx_p">We define <math id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="I(LLM,q)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>I</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>L</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>M</mi></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi>q</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">I(LLM,q)</annotation></semantics></math> to represent the “Insight Score” for a given LLM based on a specific quality indicator <math id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p3.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="q" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>q</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">q</annotation></semantics></math> as follows:</p>
<table id="Sx3.E1" class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table">

<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math id="Sx3.E1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="I(LLM,q)=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{r_{\text{target}_{i}}|_{q}-1}{n}" display="block" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>I</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>L</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>M</mi></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi>q</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>m</mi></mfrac><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><munderover><mo movablelimits="false">∑</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mi>m</mi></munderover><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><msub><mtext>target</mtext><mi>i</mi></msub></msub><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mi>q</mi></msub><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mi>n</mi></mfrac></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">I(LLM,q)=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{r_{\text{target}_{i}}|_{q}-1}{n}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td>
<td rowspan="1" class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_eqno ltx_align_middle ltx_align_right"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(1)</span></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
<p id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p3.2" class="ltx_p">Intuitively, <math id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="I(LLM,q)\in[0,1]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>I</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>L</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>M</mi></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi>q</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">I(LLM,q)\in[0,1]</annotation></semantics></math>. If all the target papers’ ideas rank first on the list, then all the <math id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p3.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r_{\text{target}_{i}}|_{q}=1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><msub><mtext>target</mtext><mi>i</mi></msub></msub><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mi>q</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r_{\text{target}_{i}}|_{q}=1</annotation></semantics></math>, so <math id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p3.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="I(LLM,q)=0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>I</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>L</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>M</mi></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi>q</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">I(LLM,q)=0</annotation></semantics></math>, indicating that the LLM is not capable of generating any research idea that surpass the quality of the target paper’s idea with respect to <math id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p3.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="q" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>q</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">q</annotation></semantics></math>. Conversely, if all the target papers’ ideas rank below all the generated ideas, that is, all the <math id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p3.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r_{\text{target}_{i}}|_{q}=n+1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><msub><mtext>target</mtext><mi>i</mi></msub></msub><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mi>q</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r_{\text{target}_{i}}|_{q}=n+1</annotation></semantics></math>, then <math id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p3.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="I(LLM,q)=1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>I</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>L</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>M</mi></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi>q</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">I(LLM,q)=1</annotation></semantics></math>, indicating that any idea generated by the LLM is superior to the target paper’s idea with respect to <math id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p3.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="q" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>q</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">q</annotation></semantics></math>.
Relative quality scoring provides a detailed and adaptable framework for assessing LLM performance, allowing for the consideration of user-defined quality indicators and offering insights into the model’s strengths and areas for improvement.</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p4" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p4.1" class="ltx_p">To ensure a fair comparison across different LLMs using <math id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p4.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="I(LLM,q)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>I</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>L</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>M</mi></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi>q</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">I(LLM,q)</annotation></semantics></math>, it’s important to generate the same number of research ideas <math id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p4.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>n</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n</annotation></semantics></math> for all compared LLMs.
Our experiments show that, for a given set of target papers, the ranking of a target paper’s research idea can vary depending on the number of generated ideas in the list.
This shifting of ranking positions can affect the Insight Scores of the LLMs.
We will further discuss the effect of <math id="Sx3.SSx3.SSSx2.p4.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>n</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n</annotation></semantics></math> has on the Insight Score in the Appendix.</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>
</section>
<section id="Sx4" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section">Experiments</h2>

<section id="Sx4.SSx1" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection">Experimental setup</h3>

<section id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx1" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">Dataset.</h4>

<div id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We curated 2,374 target papers and their corresponding 29,408 reference papers. The total number of filtered reference papers is 23,460.
We will present the descriptive statistics of the number of references a target paper has, with and without our filtering process in the Appendix.</p>
</div>

The table shows semantic similarity (80th percentile BERTScore (F1 score)), and the idea overlap (80th percentile LLM similarity rating) between the generated research idea and the target paper abstract, and the novelty and feasibility Insight Scores for various LLMs in high and low resource settings. Bold scores represent the highest score of a given metric.</figcaption>

</section>
<section id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx2" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">Models.</h4>

<div id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">To evaluate LLMs’ capability of generating research ideas, we test the latest version of several most popular commercial and open-sourced LLM series with different sizes: Meta LLama Series <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib19" title="" class="ltx_ref">Touvron et al. 2023</a>)</cite>, Google Gemini Series <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib17" title="" class="ltx_ref">Reid et al. 2024</a>)</cite>, and OpenAI GPT Series <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib14" title="" class="ltx_ref">OpenAI 2023</a>)</cite>. All of these models were trained on data with cutoff dates before January 1, 2024, so the target papers published after January 1, 2024 guarantee a fair comparison by avoiding the data leakage issue.</p>
</div>
<figure id="Sx4.F3" class="ltx_figure"><span class="ltx_inline-block"><svg id="Sx4.F3.pic1" class="ltx_picture" height="228.04" overflow="visible" version="1.1" viewBox="0 0 700 228.04" width="700"><g style="--ltx-stroke-color:#000000;--ltx-fill-color:#000000;" fill="#000000" stroke="#000000" stroke-width="0.4pt" transform="translate(0,228.04) matrix(1 0 0 -1 0 0)"><g style="--ltx-fill-color:#000000;" fill="#000000" fill-opacity="1.0"><path style="stroke:none" d="M 0 5.91 L 0 222.14 C 0 225.4 2.64 228.04 5.91 228.04 L 694.09 228.04 C 697.36 228.04 700 225.4 700 222.14 L 700 5.91 C 700 2.64 697.36 0 694.09 0 L 5.91 0 C 2.64 0 0 2.64 0 5.91 Z"></path></g><g style="--ltx-fill-color:#D9D9D9;" fill="#D9D9D9" fill-opacity="1.0"><path style="stroke:none" d="M 1.97 5.91 L 1.97 187.33 L 698.03 187.33 L 698.03 5.91 C 698.03 3.73 696.27 1.97 694.09 1.97 L 5.91 1.97 C 3.73 1.97 1.97 3.73 1.97 5.91 Z"></path></g><g fill-opacity="1.0" transform="matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 21.65 195.93)"><foreignObject style="--ltx-fo-width:47.46em;--ltx-fo-height:1.89em;--ltx-fo-depth:0.19em;font-size:10pt;" height="28.9" overflow="visible" transform="matrix(1 0 0 -1 0 26.21)" width="656.71"><span class="ltx_foreignobject_container"><span class="ltx_foreignobject_content">
<span id="Sx4.F3.pic1.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_minipage ltx_align_bottom" style="width:47.46em;">
<span id="Sx4.F3.pic1.1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Sx4.F3.pic1.1.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#FFFFFF;">Prompt template used to obtain LLM similarity rating to measure the overlap of ideas between the generated research idea and the target paper.</span></span>
</span></span></span></foreignObject></g><g fill-opacity="1.0" transform="matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 21.65 16.47)"><foreignObject style="--ltx-fo-width:47.46em;--ltx-fo-height:11.49em;--ltx-fo-depth:0.19em;font-size:10pt;" height="161.74" overflow="visible" transform="matrix(1 0 0 -1 0 159.05)" width="656.71"><span class="ltx_foreignobject_container"><span class="ltx_foreignobject_content">
<span id="Sx4.F3.pic1.2" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_minipage ltx_align_bottom" style="width:47.46em;">
<span id="Sx4.F3.pic1.2.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Sx4.F3.pic1.2.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#000000;">You are an expert in understanding and analyzing scientific content. Your task is to evaluate the degree of overlap between the ideas presented in a hypothesis and the abstract of a scientific paper. Please read both the hypothesis and the abstract carefully. Then, rate the overlap on a scale of 1 to 10, where 1 indicates minimal or no overlap, and 10 indicates a perfect or nearly perfect overlap. Provide a brief explanation for your rating. 
<br class="ltx_break"></span></span>
<span id="Sx4.F3.pic1.2.2" class="ltx_p"><span id="Sx4.F3.pic1.2.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#000000;">Hypothesis: <code id="Sx4.F3.pic1.2.2.1.1" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{generated_research_idea}</code> 
<br class="ltx_break"></span></span>
<span id="Sx4.F3.pic1.2.3" class="ltx_p"><span id="Sx4.F3.pic1.2.3.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#000000;">Abstract: <code id="Sx4.F3.pic1.2.3.1.1" class="ltx_verbatim ltx_font_typewriter">{target_paper_abstract}</code> 
<br class="ltx_break"></span></span>
<span id="Sx4.F3.pic1.2.4" class="ltx_p"><span id="Sx4.F3.pic1.2.4.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#000000;">Rating:
On a scale of 1-10, rate the overlap between the ideas in the hypothesis and the abstract. 
<br class="ltx_break"></span></span>
<span id="Sx4.F3.pic1.2.5" class="ltx_p"><span id="Sx4.F3.pic1.2.5.1" class="ltx_text" style="--ltx-fg-color:#000000;">Explanation:
In one sentence, provide a brief explanation for your rating, mentioning the key points of overlap and any significant differences you observed.</span></span>
</span></span></span></foreignObject></g></g></svg></span>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure">Figure 3: </span>Prompt template to obtain LLM similarity rating.</figcaption>
</figure>
</section>
<section id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx3" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">Baseline Comparison Metrics.</h4>

<div id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">To demonstrate the advantage of the Insight Score, we compare it with two similarity metrics: Semantic similarity and idea overlap. BERTScore (F1 score) <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib27" title="" class="ltx_ref">Zhang et al. 2019</a>)</cite> is used to measure semantic similarity.
The practical upper limit of BERTScore is task dependent. To find this upper limit, we compute the BERTScore of the target papers’ abstracts and their LLM-summarized research ideas and obtain an average score of 0.718. Although BERTScore ranges from 0 to 1, 0.718 is our practical upper limit.</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx3.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx3.p2.1" class="ltx_p">The LLM similarity rating, which uses GPT-4o, measures the overlap in ideas between a generated research idea and the abstract of its target paper. It outputs a rating between 0 to 10 for the overlap in ideas, along with an explanation of the rating. The prompt template for the LLM similarity rating is shown in Figure <a href="#Sx4.F3" title="Figure 3 ‣ Models. ‣ Experimental setup ‣ Experiments ‣ IdeaBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models for Research Idea Generation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>.
Of the <math id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx3.p2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>n</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n</annotation></semantics></math> generated research ideas, the one with the highest semantic similarity is considered when measuring idea overlap.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx4" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">Low and High Resource Scenarios.</h4>

<div id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx4.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx4.p1.1" class="ltx_p">To account for the high cost of inputting numerous reference paper abstracts into an LLM, we consider low and high resource scenarios to assess the capabilities of LLMs when researchers face computational constraints and when they do not.
In the low resource scenario, an LLM inputs five references filtered by our filtering method introduced earlier.
In the high resource scenario, an LLM inputs all unfiltered references, with the exception of GPT-3.5 Turbo, which truncates references that cannot fit into its context window.
We discuss how reference filtering and the number of references affect generating research ideas in the ablation study.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx5" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">Different <math id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx5.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="q" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>q</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">q</annotation></semantics></math> Scenarios.</h4>

<div id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx5.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx1.SSSx5.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We will measure two types of quality indicators:
feasibility and novelty. Feasibility of the ideas may be limited by the target paper because the target paper’s idea has been verified by human researchers, whereas the generated ideas have not. The novelty of the generated ideas may exceed those ideas in the target papers. Given a set of reference papers, there is no guarantee that the target paper exhibits the highest level of novelty. It is possible that better ideas can be generated from the same set of references.</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>
<section id="Sx4.SSx2" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection">Main Results</h3>

<div id="Sx4.SSx2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We benchmark LLMs in low and high resource scenarios to assess their ability to generate research ideas. We use semantic similarity and idea overlap to measure their similarity to target papers. We also evaluate research idea generation based on two quality indicators: novelty and feasibility, using the Insight Score. In the implementation, we generate <math id="Sx4.SSx2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n=3" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n=3</annotation></semantics></math> research ideas per query.
The results for semantic similarity, idea overlap, and the
novelty and feasibility Insight Scores are in Table <a href="#Sx4.T1" title="Table 1 ‣ Dataset. ‣ Experimental setup ‣ Experiments ‣ IdeaBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models for Research Idea Generation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>. Below we will answer specific questions through the analysis of the results.</p>
</div>
<section id="Sx4.SSx2.SSSx1" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">Can LLMs generate research ideas?</h4>

<div id="Sx4.SSx2.SSSx1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx2.SSSx1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Most LLMs can generate research ideas that align well with their target papers. Table <a href="#Sx4.T1" title="Table 1 ‣ Dataset. ‣ Experimental setup ‣ Experiments ‣ IdeaBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models for Research Idea Generation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a> shows high semantic similarity and idea overlap with target papers for most models, with GPT-4o Mini (high resource) followed by GPT-3.5 Turbo (high resource) exhibiting the highest scores. Generally, we observe that the high resource scenario generates ideas that have higher similarity scores than in the low resource scenario. These similarity scores demonstrate alignment with target paper ideas, indicating that LLMs, although they cannot see the target papers, can comprehend the background information enough to generate research ideas similar to those generated by human researchers.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="Sx4.SSx2.SSSx2" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">How well can LLMs generate novel research ideas?</h4>

<div id="Sx4.SSx2.SSSx2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx2.SSSx2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Most LLMs are capable to generate research ideas that are just as, if not, more novel than their target papers’ research ideas. Any Insight Score greater than 0.5 indicates that most generated research ideas are ranked above their target papers’ research ideas, concerning a quality indicator. Most of the LLMs yield novelty Insight Scores of over 0.6 with GPT-4o (high resource) having the highest score of 0.766. This means that for most LLMs, most of their generated research ideas are potentially more novel than the research idea of their target paper.
This is significant as it demonstrates the potential of LLMs to drive scientific discovery forward with new and innovative research ideas.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="Sx4.SSx2.SSSx3" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">How well can LLMs generate feasible research ideas? </h4>

<div id="Sx4.SSx2.SSSx3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx2.SSSx3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Most LLMs generate research ideas with lower feasibility than their target papers. As shown in Table <a href="#Sx4.T1" title="Table 1 ‣ Dataset. ‣ Experimental setup ‣ Experiments ‣ IdeaBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models for Research Idea Generation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>, these ideas generally have low feasibility Insight Scores. GPT-3.5 Turbo (high resource) and Gemini 1.5 Pro (low and high resource) achieve the highest scores, yet all LLMs score below 0.5, indicating that most of their ideas rank lower in feasibility compared to their target papers. Although LLMs can produce novel ideas, their feasibility often remains inferior to human-generated research ideas.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="Sx4.SSx2.SSSx4" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">What is the relationship between generating novel and feasible research ideas?</h4>

<div id="Sx4.SSx2.SSSx4.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx2.SSSx4.p1.1" class="ltx_p">For all LLMs, there is a gap between the novelty and the feasibility of their research ideas. Table <a href="#Sx4.T1" title="Table 1 ‣ Dataset. ‣ Experimental setup ‣ Experiments ‣ IdeaBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models for Research Idea Generation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a> shows that with the exception of GPT-3.5 Turbo (high resource), all models yield higher novelty Insight Scores than feasibility Insight Scores. The intensity of this gap varies across models. GPT-4o and the LLama 3.1 models exhibit the largest gaps, while GPT-3.5 Turbo, GPT-4o Mini, and the Gemini series of models have smaller gaps. This indicates a general trend toward a trade-off between generating research ideas that are more novel or feasible, with the degree of the gap varying across models. This trade-off is intuitive, as research ideas that propose pursuing more novel, unexplored approaches may be less feasible to implement than ideas suggesting more incremental contributions.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="Sx4.SSx2.SSSx5" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">Can reference filtering help lower-capacity models produce more novel research ideas?</h4>

<div id="Sx4.SSx2.SSSx5.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx2.SSSx5.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Reference filtering plays a crucial role in enabling lower-capacity models to generate more novel research ideas. As shown in Table <a href="#Sx4.T1" title="Table 1 ‣ Dataset. ‣ Experimental setup ‣ Experiments ‣ IdeaBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models for Research Idea Generation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>, GPT-3.5 Turbo and Llama 3.1 70B-Instruct, both smaller models in their respective families, yield higher novelty Insight Scores in the low resource scenario compared to the high resource scenario. Due to their lower capacity, these models are likely distracted by irrelevant references from target papers with fewer total references since most target papers have less than 16 references.
Thus, reference filtering becomes essential to help smaller models focus on the most relevant ideas, boosting their ability to generate more novel research ideas.</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>
<section id="Sx4.SSx3" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection">Ablation Study</h3>

</table>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table">Table 2: </span>Comparison of semantic similarity and idea overlap scores for research ideas generated by GPT-4o Mini with filtered and unfiltered references. Underlined scores are higher when compared to their filtered/unfiltered counterpart. Bold values are the highest for each measure.</figcaption>
</figure>
<div id="Sx4.SSx3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">For the ablation study we gathered a subset of target papers that have at least 15 references and generated research ideas using GPT-4o Mini <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib14" title="" class="ltx_ref">OpenAI 2023</a>)</cite> with a varying number of filtered and unfiltered references.
We evaluated the ideas generated using semantic similarity and idea overlap.
We also calculate the Insight Scores for novelty and feasibility, which indicate the quality of the research idea with respect to novelty and feasibility. The results of these evaluations are presented in Figure <a href="#Sx4.F4" title="Figure 4 ‣ Ablation Study ‣ Experiments ‣ IdeaBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models for Research Idea Generation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a> and Table <a href="#Sx4.T2" title="Table 2 ‣ Ablation Study ‣ Experiments ‣ IdeaBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models for Research Idea Generation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>.</p>
</div>
<section id="Sx4.SSx3.SSSx1" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">Effectiveness of the Insight Score.</h4>

<div id="Sx4.SSx3.SSSx1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx3.SSSx1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We explore the effectiveness of our Insight Score by applying it to the quality indicators novelty and feasibility. Figure <a href="#Sx4.F4" title="Figure 4 ‣ Ablation Study ‣ Experiments ‣ IdeaBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models for Research Idea Generation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a> shows how the Insight Score for novelty and feasibility evolves as we incorporate more references to generate research ideas.
As the number of references increases, whether filtered or unfiltered, the Insight Score for novelty also increases. This shows that LLM-generated ideas tend to display more novelty when they are generated with more references.</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx4.SSx3.SSSx1.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx3.SSSx1.p2.1" class="ltx_p">However, the feasibility of these LLM-generated ideas do not follow the same pattern. As shown in Figure <a href="#Sx4.F4" title="Figure 4 ‣ Ablation Study ‣ Experiments ‣ IdeaBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models for Research Idea Generation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a>, increasing the number of references leads the feasibility Insight Score to plateau at a low level, regardless of whether the references are filtered. Notably, the feasibility Insight Scores remain consistently lower than the novelty Insight Scores, except in the case where the research ideas are generated with only a single reference. In this instance, the novelty Insight Score is low.</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx4.SSx3.SSSx1.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx3.SSSx1.p3.1" class="ltx_p">These findings demonstrate the utility of our Insight Score in capturing complex patterns that similarity metrics may overlook. Our results demonstrate that once a certain threshold of novelty is surpassed, the feasibility of generated ideas tends to decline and stabilize at a lower level. This observation supports the trade-off between novelty and feasibility identified in our main results, further highlighting the importance of our Insight Score in assessing the dynamics between these two important quality indicators.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="Sx4.SSx3.SSSx2" class="ltx_subsubsection">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsubsection">Effect of reference filtering on generated research ideas.</h4>

<div id="Sx4.SSx3.SSSx2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx3.SSSx2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">The alignment of the LLM generated research ideas to the target papers improves as the number of references increases. Specifically, filtering plays a critical role in enhancing the similarity of the generated ideas to the target paper when not all references are provided. Table <a href="#Sx4.T2" title="Table 2 ‣ Ablation Study ‣ Experiments ‣ IdeaBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models for Research Idea Generation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a> shows that when all references are not available, filtered references lead to more alignment compared to unfiltered ones. Irrelevant information can cause the LLM’s output to diverge when given limited context. By filtering out less relevant references, the LLM is guided to produce ideas that are more closely aligned with the target paper.</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx4.SSx3.SSSx2.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx3.SSSx2.p2.1" class="ltx_p">However, when all references are available, the benefits of filtering are lost. Table <a href="#Sx4.T2" title="Table 2 ‣ Ablation Study ‣ Experiments ‣ IdeaBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models for Research Idea Generation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a> shows that with all references available, unfiltered references produce the most aligned research ideas. This indicates that with sufficient references, the LLM is better equipped to ignore irrelevant information and leverage the comprehensive knowledge provided by all unfiltered references, resulting in research ideas that are most similar to the target papers.</p>
</div>
<div id="Sx4.SSx3.SSSx2.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx4.SSx3.SSSx2.p3.1" class="ltx_p">Overall, using unfiltered references tends to produce the most aligned research ideas when all references are available. However, in scenarios with limited references, reference filtering is beneficial. This is especially relevant given the resource-intensive nature of generating ideas with LLMs as well as the input constraints of some models.</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>
</section>
<section id="Sx5" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section">Conclusion</h2>

<div id="Sx5.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Sx5.p1.1" class="ltx_p">In this work, we introduced <span id="Sx5.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">IdeaBench</span>, a benchmark system for evaluating LLMs’ ability to generate research ideas based on user-defined quality indicators. The dataset is constructed by emulating human researchers’ literature review process, providing grounded contextualization for LLMs to generate research ideas. For evaluation, we proposed the “Insight Score”, a metric that surpasses similarity-based measures by capturing nuanced, user-specified quality indicators through personalized quality ranking and relative quality scoring. This work can serve as the cornerstone for academia to build up confidence in leveraging LLMs to accelerate ideation in scientific discovery.</p>
</div>
</section>

<section id="A1" class="ltx_appendix">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_appendix"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix A </span>Appendix</h2>

<section id="A1.SSx1" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection">Code and Dataset Availability</h3>

<div id="A1.SSx1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A1.SSx1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Due to the complexity of our dataset, we combined our dataset with all the code needed to generate our results and made them available at: <span class="ltx_ref ltx_nolink ltx_url ltx_ref_self">https://anonymous.4open.science/r/IdeaBench-2747/</span></p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="A1.SSx2" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection">Implementation Details</h3>

<div id="A1.SSx2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A1.SSx2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We describe the resources used to generate and evaluate research ideas. We used various API services to generate research ideas and to evaluate them. Additionally, we employed accelerated hardware to compute semantic similarity scores between generated research ideas and their corresponding target paper abstracts.</p>
</div>
<div id="A1.SSx2.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A1.SSx2.p2.1" class="ltx_p">The OpenAI API service <span id="footnote1" class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">1</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">1</sup>
              <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">1</span>
              
              
            More details about the OpenAI API service can be found here: <span class="ltx_ref ltx_nolink ltx_url ltx_ref_self">https://platform.openai.com/docs/overview</span></span></span></span> was employed to generate research ideas that used the OpenAI suite of models. The service was also used for extracting research ideas from target paper abstracts and evaluating research ideas with the LLM similarity rating and the Insight Score. To generate research ideas with the Gemini family of LLMs, Google AI’s API service <span id="footnote2" class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">2</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">2</sup>
              <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">2</span>
              
              
            More details about the Google AI API service can be found here: <span class="ltx_ref ltx_nolink ltx_url ltx_ref_self">https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/api-key</span></span></span></span> was used. To generate research ideas with the Llama 3.1 family of LLMs, DeepInfra’s API service <span id="footnote3" class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">3</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">3</sup>
              <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">3</span>
              
              
            More details about the DeepInfra’s API service can be found here: <span class="ltx_ref ltx_nolink ltx_url ltx_ref_self">https://deepinfra.com/</span></span></span></span> was used.</p>
</div>
<div id="A1.SSx2.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A1.SSx2.p3.1" class="ltx_p">To evaluate the semantic similarity between research ideas and their corresponding target paper abstracts, we computed BERTScores using one NVIDIA A6000 48GB GPU. This hardware allowed for the efficient computation of BERTScores.</p>
</div>
</section>
